Summary

Agatha Harkness is a retired Olympic figure skater - the most decorated in history. At nearly 45, she now runs her family business in Houston, Texas: Harkness Oil. She's also a (now former) assistant coach in the NHL.

Soon-to-be twenty-five-year-old Rio Vidal is an elite hockey player, just finishing the inaugural PWHL season, and with special permission from the commissioner, joining the NHL draft. She's a decorated Olympian herself, keeps up and surpasses the boys, and is ready to show the world what women can do.

When Agatha's Aunt Lilia buys the Houston Comets, the worst team in the league, Agatha is tasked with rebuilding the franchise and creating the dynasty team Houston deserves. Drafting Rio upon her Aunt's insistence is just the beginning of the multitude of changes. Sparks will fly, but not all of them good. After all, the pressure the two women are under is almost insurmountable, and they’re under too much scrutiny to indulge in something forbidden.

It doesn't help that once Rio is actually coached by Agatha, they hate each other.

    Chapter 1: Holdin' Out For A Hero

    Chapter Summary Agatha Harkness, CEO of Harkness Oil and former Olympic figure skater, is presented with her dream job.

    But first, she must assemble a team.

    Rio Vidal, fresh off the first season of the PWHL, becomes the first woman to enter the NHL draft and attend the combine.

    Chapter Notes Welcome to the Hockey AU that we (Ellen/AgathaLovesRio, and Hannah/CrystalsandLavender) have been planning and researching (Ellen) since January!

    We are so excited to bring this to you! We've tried hard to make this somewhat realistic, though some things obviously aren't.

    We are using some real people, sports personalities mainly, but as background characters mainly. So, not an RPF, just cameos. Updates will be weekly(ish) 💜 35 chapters is an estimate. The count will likely go up as we will probably need to split a few chapters. Welcome the journey, and buckle up for safety. 🫡 The authors are native Texans and therefore speak fluent cornbread. You can expect to find several southernisms sprinkled throughout the fic.

    You can find us on tumblr! DeathbyLesbianWitches (AgathaLovesRio) and Hannah-0730 (CrystalsandLavender)

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Wednesday, May 29th, 2024 Houston, Texas

Minute Maid Park, NRG Stadium, The Toyota Center, the Brown Convention Center, and the Aquarium all faced the scrutiny of Agatha’s gaze as she worked her tongue against her cheek and pinched the bridge of her nose in an attempt to stave off her impending headache. Her long fingers were perfectly manicured, painted black, and adorned with rings of varying shapes and sizes. Her fitted burgundy suit was crisp, hugging and accentuating every curve perfectly.     Friday, May 31st, 2024 St. Paul, Minnesota Rio Vidal turned the TV off after the press conference and tossed the remote onto her bed in her mother’s house. Since finding out that the Houston Comets had a new owner and coach, she had been trying to learn everything she could about them. She knew the Comets had the first pick, so there had already been a good chance she would be drafted by them before. With Harkness as coach? It was almost a guarantee.

    Or at least she thought it was. Houston needed a center, and that’s what she was. She had an amazing career and was a three-time Olympian. It was hard to make hockey her career as a woman, but she did her best. The past month, however, had been a whirlwind of activity.     Rio took that personally, and in the next press conference she was in, she called him out by saying, “Challenge accepted, Mr. Stark. Any man who wants to try and play with us is welcome.” It was as much of a polite ‘fuck you’ as it was a gauntlet.

    “What’d you think?” Her friend asked immediately.

If she could fluster a professional, then Rio didn’t stand a chance during her interview. What if she were invited to dinner? Several teams had the habit of doing that for top prospects. She might not have any intentions of getting involved with her coach, but Agatha was an attractive, mean, charismatic femme, twenty years her senior, and Rio was only human. Unfortunately.

    Calm down, Vidal. The other coaches will be there. She thought to herself. It was times like these when she needed her mom, but she knew Mami was working the night shift at the hospital. It didn’t make sense to bother her. Instead, she forced herself to shower and try to sleep before her week of being judged by a shit ton of arrogant men and one brilliant woman began. She had a feeling that there wasn’t enough sleep in the world to prepare her for this.

    Chapter 3: Take A Chance On Me

    Chapter Summary The draft is here, and other than a run-in with Tony, everything goes according to plan.

    Chapter Notes Hey guys!!! Look at us, timing the draft chapter with the actual NHL draft!

    We are having a blast writing this!

    You can find us on tumblr at @deathbylesbianwitches and @hannah- See the end of the chapter for more notes Monday, June 24th, 2024 Saint Paul, Minnesota

    Rio felt as though her entire life had flipped upside down the moment she landed back in Saint Paul.     Let’s see what the cards hold in Vegas.

    Thursday Morning, June 27th, 2024 Maximoff Residence Houston, Texas

The practice facilities were still under renovation, and she was working six times as hard as anyone else, playing coach, general manager, and agent, yet she still managed to make time for her son. She always would.

    Fortunately, Agatha Harkness was far from average and was putting her coping skills to very good use.

    The main problem, however, was that she was unable to invite her top prospects to visit the facilities and practice with a few of her players, whom she knew would be staying for the next season.

You won’t have the time or the energy. You are all fighting for a spot on a twenty-three-man roster.”

    No matter what everyone else said.

    August 13th, 2024 Nicky’s Birthday Harkness Residence

    August in Texas.

Sitting in the NICU, staring at her beautiful boy, born at twenty-eight weeks, weighing two pounds and measuring fourteen inches long. The purple butterfly prominent on his incubator.

Ironic, since her mother was the reason she had gone into preterm labor, her actions supposedly carried out in the name of said god.

    For 103 minutes and twenty-two seconds, she had cried out “please let her live,” as surgeons tried to save her severely underdeveloped daughter, while simultaneously begging the fabled man: “please give me more time, I need more time,” for the son who, while stronger, was still in a fight for his life. She sobbed, her abdomen radiating pain with every cracked sound as the family that mattered surrounded her. For what supposedly noble god would ever allow a coffin that was no bigger than a loaf of bread to exist? It was cruel and inhumane to force Agatha to bury half of her bruised and battered heart.

    Layla Elise Harkness August 13th, 2017 - August 13th, 2017 “If Love Could’ve Saved You, You Would’ve Lived Forever”

    “We’ll see how you feel after the game.” Agatha smiled, all teeth. “The network wants to mic you up,” Brooks told her hesitantly. “The network can fuck itself.” There was no way in hell she, or her players, were wearing mics. The things she said during games were not primetime appropriate.

    “We’re miccing up the starting six, and a few others. Plus the other coaches.” Cotton informed.

    “The fuck you are!”

    Logan leaned over and whispered in her ear. “I’ll show you how to turn them off.”

    “Fine.” She grimaced, knowing she wouldn’t win this particular argument. “Great, now, we need to ask you a few pre-game questions.” Cotton smiled, handing her a microphone.

    Smoothing her tie, she flung her hair behind her. “Fine. Let’s get it over with.”

    Rio wasn’t fond of being fitted with a mic; she never was, but she understood why it was being done. Tapping her foot as she sat on the bench before they took the ice for warm-ups, she took a moment to take everything in. The game was sold out, and according to Birdie, there was an extremely important guest in one of the suites.

    Rio had played in front of presidents, government officials from all countries, royalty, and celebrities; it wasn’t unfamiliar territory to her. She was an Olympian, and world leaders supported their countries. For this? A Houston Comets game? She expected it was some A-list celebrity. Still, Birdie had seemed serious about it, and Rio couldn’t quite pinpoint why. Standing, she put her helmet on, now wearing the mic, and headed to the ice with her teammates to prepare for the game.

    Taking the ice, she looked into the stands where fans of both teams were starting to take their seats. Peter skated to her side, a smile on his face and a tear in his eye.

    “Parker, the game hasn’t started yet; you can’t start crying now.” “No, it’s just. My dad always said he would sit behind the glass at the player’s bench, not up in some family box. I told Coach H that, and look.” She followed where he was pointing, and sure enough, behind their bench, sat Aunt May, smiling and waving to both of them. Rio’s Mami was next to her, as was Nicky. The entire section was filled with their biggest supporters. Their families. Lilia and Wanda hadn’t taken their seats yet, though Rio knew Lilia had a speech before the game and Wanda was probably with her wife.

    What slightly surprised Rio was to see Cassie, Scott’s daughter, there with her mom and stepdad. Family truly was a team priority, and it made her hate Agatha slightly less in the moment as she waved to Alice and Lorna. Jen, of course, was working.

    After practicing a few shots, plays, and passing drills, Loki blew the whistle for them to stop and leave the ice, Agatha watching them from the tunnel. “Locker room, water, stretch,” Scott instructed, waving to Cassie one last time.

    “Mics off for now,” Nat instructed.

    She was ready for a lot of things; hockey was only one of them. “Yes, Agatha.”

    “Good boy, now, there are a lot of eyes on you tonight, and it’s going to be loud—music and announcers over the soundsystem. I need you to focus on the ice and your surroundings, and make sure you’re communicating with your team.”

    There wasn’t time to argue, nor was it the place. As much as Rio hated to admit it, her head coach was right.

    Reaching for her shoulder pads where the mic was placed, Agatha changed the channel it was set to. Rio involuntarily held her breath during the contact. With Agatha’s hand so close to her face, she had been tempted to turn into it.

I won’t say anything that could be construed as cheating, just words of encouragement.” Patting the shoulder pad, she slipped past Rio and into the locker room to begin her speech. The thought of having Agatha’s voice in her ear for the entire game made her shiver.

    Rio had to get her head back into the impending game; she didn't want Agatha throwing her off like this. She wasn’t even sure if her head coach knew the effect she was having on her. Shaking the thoughts from her head, she headed into the locker room to listen to the pregame speeches.

    After her pregame interview, Agatha was met by Birdie outside of the announcer booth.

    “She’s here and would like to see you first, as well as the other coaches.” Her assistant informed her in a neutral tone.

If Agatha had found a way to donate more money to her campaign than legally allowed, that was their business. Most people, upon seeing the Governor and CEO together, often thought they were twins or sisters. However, the two powerful women didn’t really see the likeness. Claire’s hair was to her shoulder blades, straight, and darker, unlike Agatha’s wild chestnut mane. Her eyes were lighter, and she was more likable than Agatha. She was a politician; she had to be. Agatha, on the other hand, could afford to be a raging bitch most of the time and took pride in that particular personality trait.

Play the way we know we can, and we’ll come out of this on top.” Agatha knew they were capable; it was whether or not her team believed it that worried her. Lilia stepped forward from where she had been standing with the assistant coaches and gave the team a genuine smile. “I don’t have much to add, but I want you all to know that I am greatly looking forward to watching you all win and, throughout the season, giving Houston the team they deserve.” Agatha nodded, sizing up her team for the night. “Also, you’ve all heard there’s a certain guest here who has caused the need for added security. My close personal friend, Governor Claire Debella, has come to cheer you on.” Turning her head, she watched as Claire entered the locker room, much like Agatha always did, like she owned the place. They had played Nashville twice during preseason—a win at home and a loss on the road. In fact, all their wins had been at home, which bode well for them.

Glancing over her shoulder, she was met with her support system and her team's family members.

    Breathe in. Breathe out. Rio repeated, entering the faceoff circle at center ice and taking her stance. The man across from her grinned. “Good to see you again, Vidal. No hard feelings when you’re rammed into a wall, right?”

    Smug fucker.

As one of the medical staff tended to the laceration, the player could feel Agatha’s eyes on her. Looking up, she caught something that looked like concern in Agatha's eyes. It was a simple cut, nothing major, and she didn’t know why her head coach actually cared. Her lack of a comeback after her smartass remark was odd, though.

Thor! That shot shouldn’t have gotten past you!” “My apologies, I did let that one slide.” Thor agreed.

Second scored on Thor today.” Brooks narrates excitedly.

The Comets need to score two goals next period to tie it and three to win!” Cotton practically vibrates with excitement.

    Agatha was furious. Several infractions hadn’t been called on Nashville, though several had been called on them. Not that they hadn’t earned them; the refs just weren't being equitable.

    “Listen up dumbfucks!” Her voice echoed off the walls of the locker room. “We’re not playing the way we know we can. This isn’t a fucking scrimmage or a preseason game. This is the real fucking deal! FUCKING PLAY LIKE IT!” Her hands were flying, and she was considering asking Birdie to fill her Stanley with whisky. Just straight Macallan. All the way to the top. If she blacked out, then she would be spared the tragedy in front of her.

NO ONE should be going into the sin bin unless it’s for a good fucking reason!” ESPN is talking shit about us, and two of the knuckleheads upstairs are taking pot shots! You want to prove the Comets are serious this year? Then get back out there and fucking PROVE IT!” She roared.

    At this rate, Agatha would have grey hair at the end of the season, and she knew it. They had to pull this out, and it was still possible in twenty minutes, if her team could get it together.

    Back on the ice, Agatha watched carefully as her team blocked multiple shots and also missed several.

    “Vidal, switch with Barton, Peter, get ready to switch with LeBeau!” Whispering low into the mic, and without looking at her, she sent words of encouragement to her star center. “Be a good boy and score some points for me, handsome.”

    Rio took the ice with a renewed purpose. As much as she hated Agatha, her words had sent orders directly to her brain, and she felt obligated to follow them. It wasn’t the right time to unpack that. With the score at one to three, Nashville, Rio had a lot of work to do.

    “Parker!” She yelled once he was on the ice and passed the puck backward to him. He took a long shot that ricocheted off the bar and into the goal. There’s one. She thought, smiling at the kid.

    “Good boy, using your words.” Agatha purred.

    Why Agatha in her head was helping, she didn’t know, but the encouragement was proving useful.

    “Give me another, handsome." She commanded.

    Rio wanted to give her something; that was for damn sure. While she couldn’t do that, she could score again.

    Four minutes left, and Rio was determined. There was traffic near the goal, creating the perfect opportunity. Rogers hit the puck away from the cluster of bodies. Rio took it, passing back to Bucky, who handled the puck up to the goal and slipped it into the corner.

    3 - 3.

    The buzzer sounded, and the whistles blew. Regulation time was over. Sudden death overtime was up next—Rio’s specialty.

    Taking a quick commercial break before the five-minute three-on-three overtime, they left the ice to return to the bench where Agatha was an odd mix of angry and…not.

    The four coaches were also deciding on who the three to play would be. Rio had practiced with various ‘death squads’. The coaches were trying to pick which one would match with Nashville the best.

    “Muerte,” Agatha stated, turning to Rio. “You did it last game. I need you to do it again.”

    “Yes, coach.” The player nodded, mesmerized by the blue eyes staring into her soul.

    Loki shook his head. “They’ve got speed, but not as much as Rio.” Scott nodded. “But they’ve pressed a lot, so change-ups.” Nat turned. “Squad two, you’re in.”

    Johnny Storm and Sam joined Rio on the ice, the team wishing them luck. Rosa gave her a thumbs up before crossing herself; Nicky jumped up and down to wave and yelled: “You can do it, Rio!”

    Taking the faceoff circle yet again, Don’t Fear the Reaper played as Logan announced her, and the jumbotron showed the three players taking the ice, followed by muerte in giant letters and an image of the grim reaper. “Feel. Listen. Block out everything but the game and my voice, Rio.” Agatha’s voice was calm, but determined, and Rio felt it everywhere. Senses sharpening, she rolled her neck, prepared for the next five minutes. “Death, huh? We’ll see about that.” The man across from her growled. “Hope you have a florist on standby.” Rio shot back as the puck fell and she immediately won it, shooting it to Sam, right as the player across from her checked her.

    Unfortunately, it wasn’t an illegal hit, and she kept going. Or tried to before icing was called on them, giving Nashville the puck. The Comets took it easily, and Rio was determined to end it right then.

    “C’mon, Seven. I’ve got women lined up to see who’s going home with me. End this.”

    Rio wasn’t sure what came over her or why a wave of anger crashed over her, but the puck came to her, and suddenly, the red lights were flashing. Rio had just scored the winning goal in a burst of rage.

    “Jealous, handsome?” Agatha cackled.

    The fucking bitch had counted on that to rile her up. The joke was on Agatha, though, because Rio was definitely not jealous; she just wanted to win the game. That’s what she told herself anyway.

    Winning their first game at home felt surreal. For Whom the Bell Tolls by Metallica played, a full stadium cheered, and on the bench, Agatha was smiling at her, really smiling. It wasn’t practiced or fake. Her grin was wide, showing her near-perfect teeth, and her eyes were sparkling with delight, turning slightly squinty as she clapped along with the crowd. It was the most beautiful thing Rio had ever witnessed.

    Agatha was proud of her team being able to pull it out. More importantly, Rio had dominated, which made it all the more worthwhile. Rio making the shot because of Agatha’s words? It thrilled her beyond measure. After their press conference and signing merch, Agatha bid her goodnights. Nicky and the twins had been taken home shortly after the game. Wanda had hugged her so tightly she thought she was going to pass out; even Mobius congratulated her. Jen was already busy with press and totaling the numbers as season ticket requests came in and excitedly updated her. Taking the arm of a woman she had flirted with while the team had handed out autographs, she moved to leave, not without a raised eyebrow from Rosa and May. She was single, goddamnit, and if she wanted to take a woman home, it was her prerogative.

    It was seeing Rio as she left that made her second-guess her decision. No. That’s the one woman you can’t have.

    “Goodnight, Vidal. See you tomorrow.” She said instead, walking out of the stadium with the raven-haired beauty she’d chosen for the night. It was a mere coincidence that the woman had the same color and length of hair as her star player.

    The high from their win was crushed two days later in Dallas. Agatha had pulled Rio after she had gone to the penalty box twice. The rookie’s temper was not helping anything. Granted, the team as a whole was doing terribly, and frustrations were running high.

    The score was 5-2; even Loki had yelled at the team. Something that never happened. The next days at practice, they skated harder and drilled more. Agatha wasn’t going to have that happen again, especially against the Stars.

    Stampede, Houston, Texas October 19th

    “Mama?” The little boy asked.

    “Yes, my love?”

He was currently doing his residency to be a pediatrician. She knew that much from Rosa. Which usually meant a high IQ…would he have? Where the hell is Birdie?

    “Coach Harkness, it’s a pleasure to meet you in person.” Fuck, even his eyes sparkled the same way Nicky’s did.

Maybe one day soon, she and Claire could spend the holidays together, especially after the divorce.     Birdie lifted her brows in response. “You want me to track down confidential information and violate HIPAA? Why?”

    Of fucking course she had. And since when did Birdie wait for permission to spend her money?

    “Oh, since the first day they met at Rookie Camp.”

    The CEO nodded, spinning on her heel and making her way to the backyard before she could overthink anything. Maybe the fresh air would do her some good.

    Rio had to hand it to her Mami: Thanksgiving at Agatha’s was a hit, and despite the multitude of odd looks the CEO had thrown her and Matteo’s way, everything was going swimmingly.

    The team’s dynamics were well on their way to healing since she and Agatha had talked, and the food and bonding were exactly what they needed to kick their asses into high gear for the rest of the season. They served the entire meal in the enormous backyard, with the team spread out over several tables.

    Rio took a bite out of her turkey, chewing thoughtfully. It was probably just because the two of them looked so much alike. When they were younger, people often mistook them for twins. Rio was sure she was overthinking it. Plus, it wasn’t like there was any malice in the looks. She could tell that she hadn’t accidentally done something to get on her coach’s bad side—those looks she was accustomed to. Loki was right; it was probably easier to leave it alone.

    Shrugging, she took a bit of the broccoli, cheese, and rice casserole. “You’re the only new face here today. That’s probably all it is.” Turning to Loki, she raised an eyebrow in slight disbelief. “I know I saw the three of you make this, and I know your husband can cook, but this is good. I didn’t know you cooked as well.”

    “Like our dear leader, I, too, have many tricks up my sleeve.” He winked at her before beginning a conversation with Matteo.

    Once everyone had finished eating, Rio began helping with the cleanup, taking stacks of plates to the kitchen to load what she could into the dishwasher.

    “You wash, I’ll dry?” A familiar raspy voice came from behind her. “The great Agatha Harkness does dishes?” She scoffed.

    Nudging the younger woman’s shoulder gently, she opened her mouth in shock. “Excuse me, I am not that spoiled. I know how to clean.” “Agatha, you have an entire household staff. I’m just surprised you would waste time on something so mundane.” She grabbed a plate from the stack, having already scraped any leftovers off all of them.

    The CEO waited patiently for Rio to begin handing her dishes to dry. “My mother made me do chores as a punishment. Said it would teach me grit and resilience. Honestly, I liked the quiet it gave me. That woman refused to do even the most basic of tasks. Nicky and the twins have chores, believe it or not.”

    Agatha’s confession blurred some of Rio’s perception of the older woman. The formidable woman had been born with oil running through her veins, yet she knew how to take care of herself and didn't rely on others to clean up after her. Around the house, at least.

    Rio kept washing dishes, handing them to Agatha, who dried them and stacked them by where they went in her enormous kitchen. It didn’t take long for their fingers to begin brushing against each other, neither of them adjusting to prevent it. Agatha could be rather tactile at times, and if they were going to keep working together, Rio had to get used to it. Even if her skin erupted in flames anytime it came into contact with Agatha’s. I also couldn’t help but overhear you call him ‘sucio’ and was wondering why. He doesn’t look dirty at all.”

    The corners of Rio’s mouth tugged into a small smile as she thought back to their childhood in Puerto Rico. “Before we moved to Minnesota, we would spend hours outside playing. Matteo always managed to get dirty, no matter what we were doing. The minute we walked back into the casita, Mami would yell, ‘¡Matteo, eres tan sucio! ¡Tendré que echarte agua con la manguera!’” She laughed, drying her hands on the towel hanging under the sink, and moved to put away the clean dishes.

    “So it stuck?” The older woman chuckled, clearly amused.

    Rio nodded, opening a cabinet to begin stowing away the tableware. The sheer amount of dishes Agatha possessed shocked her.

    “Nicky and the twins will do that and get absolutely filthy. It’s why both houses are equipped with outdoor showers. They come in handy for swimming, too.” Agatha took the glasses and began placing them on the shelves slightly above Rio, her heels giving her the advantage. “Ags, why do you have all this? You can’t possibly use it all regularly.”

    Rio had called her ‘Ags’. Something she had never done before, and Agatha’s breath hitched. Why had she reacted that way? People called her Aggie and Ags all the fucking time. Because it’s Rio, her inner voice answered. She was pressed against Rio’s side, putting away the fucking dishes, and it felt as if the damn crab from The Little Mermaid was singing Kiss the Girl. Not today, Satan.

    Moving to another part of the kitchen to put away casserole dishes, she gathered her thoughts. “I do entertain. Quite often, actually. Celebrities, politicians, global leaders. I host intimate charity events occasionally, or parties here. I’m the owner and CEO of the world’s largest oil company. I do have to play the part at times.”

    Closing the cabinet, Rio turned, gazing at Agatha with a slight frown. “Does it ever become too much?”

    “I was raised in it. I like to keep busy, and I do.” What the fuck? Agatha was usually not candid, and she was being entirely too open. How the fuck had no one come in and interrupted them? She had come entirely too close to doing something stupid multiple times. When Rio asked about Matteo, Agatha had almost told her the real reason she couldn’t take her eyes off the man. It was time to shut ‘Ask Agatha ' down for the day.

    Clapping her hands in front of her before Rio could respond, she turned her head toward the doorway and the safety of others. “Well, we’re done; let’s go watch some powder puffs grunt over a pigskin, shall we?” As she exited the kitchen, Rio on her heels, she almost collided with Birdie, who was coming straight toward them, out of breath.

    “Birdie? What’s wrong?” Agatha grabbed her by the upper arms to steady her.

    Her eyes were wide, and she looked as if she’d seen a ghost. “I just got an update about Stark and Darkhold.”

    Yeah. Agatha thought, it does become too much.
